Ada <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1076\" data-words=\"1076\">braced</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-6712\" data-words=\"6712\">rail</a>. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-4571\" data-words=\"4571,4568\">Its</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1158\" data-words=\"1158\">bulk</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7488\" data-words=\"7488\">shifted</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8429\" data-words=\"8429\">then</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3954\" data-words=\"3954\">held</a>. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3344\" data-words=\"3344\">For</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-5722\" data-words=\"5722\">one</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7338\" data-words=\"7338\">second</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7157\" data-words=\"7157\">room</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7360\" data-words=\"7360\">seemed</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-6085\" data-words=\"6085\">perfectly</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7969\" data-words=\"7969\">still</a>.</p><p id=\"chapter-0142-p21\"><a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8429\" data-words=\"8429\">Then</a> Rowan <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7270\" data-words=\"7270\">said</a>, “<a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-2098\" data-words=\"2098\">Damn</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> waistcoat. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1275\" data-words=\"1275\">Carry</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3891\" data-words=\"3891\">her</a>.”</p><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"140\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"142\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x016c02","title":"A Runway with No Middle","volume":"婚后外传：公爵背后裂开的马甲","anchor":"chapter-0143","paragraphs":["Cassian lifted the apprentice off the broken runway. His waistcoat split down the back. The girl, who had been trying not to cry, began to laugh instead. Mara loved the spontaneity of the sound. Her husband looked at her. “One word, and I shall send you the bill.”","“Your physique is a public responsibility.”","The promoter called the breakage a triviality. Ada called a stage without support a weak point. Its severity required a carpenter, not a change of adjectives. She refused to let him pressure anyone onto it. The ultimate show might be delayed; a student's safety would not be traded for predictability in his schedule.","Two parallel beams should have supported the centre. One was gone. Prudence had required checking both, plus the fixings, before a rehearsal. The practicality of that rule was now painfully clear. Notwithstanding his excuses, the promoter could not call the danger a triviality and expect the apprentices to trust his judgment.","A donated fabric roll lay across the missing support's old position. Its weight had been shifted without checking the structure. Inertia kept it moving when a volunteer tried to stop it, while the slope added acceleration. Cassian helped guide it safely to the floor. Nobody should have to test their stamina against falling scenery.","Mara examined the promoter's fabric certificate. Its trademark stamp resembled the maker's original, but the spacing lacked symmetry. The signature's tint was wrong too. She requested the actual supplier's name. A rarity could command a high price; the claim needed more than an impressive label. Truthfulness mattered before someone promised a donor an antique gown.","The prevalence of unsupported claims made Ada angry. This latest label's deceptiveness was obvious once the pages lay together. She did not regard a mistake as a sin. A wise response would admit it and preserve the originals. Instead, the promoter randomly blamed the nearest assistant. His account did not fit with the timing of his own messages.","The thinness of the paper made the old stamp visible underneath. Its mismatched tint was similar to a common reprint, though that did not establish its origin. A succession of small alterations had changed the document. The deceit's efficacy depended on viewers accepting the whole before looking at any part.","The promoter's aggressiveness increased. He said all certificates were essentially similar. Ada rejected that vague similarity. A receipt and a material test had complementary functions. Neither guaranteed the other's validity. The deceptive label's simplicity had been its strength: nobody had thought to ask.","Outside, the port was populous and hot. Residents queued for tickets beneath luxuriant trees. The housing crisis made the fundraiser urgent, but urgency did not endorse arrogance. The organizer asked the tourism industry sponsors for a short delay. They gave it voluntarily. Their commitment to the guild survived the loss of a photo opportunity.","The assistant who had shifted the fabric confessed. She had followed a careless instruction and then been told to deny it. Mara thanked her truthfulness. That did not remove the consequence; it made a sensible response possible. A promise of perpetual punishment would only make the next apprentice afraid to speak.","Mara wanted to awaken her confidence, not reward an unsafe action. She asked the assistant to describe the instruction. The girl could testify to what she heard; she could not know every motive behind it. This careful distinction seemed to overwhelm her less than a demand to judge the whole affair. Cassian gave her a chair and water.","Transferability of the workshop patterns was still unresolved. Ada's aspiration was a library apprentices could use, not a demonstration they had to pay to watch. Its complexity needed checking. The software agreement appeared questionable, and she refused to be rushed by the promoter's competitiveness.","Ada had put the safety criteria beside the sewing instructions. Their suitability could be checked openly. The promoter complained that such cautiousness would reduce productivity. Ada answered that damaged people and damaged gowns were not useful output. Her realism had the rigour of someone who had seen both.","Their uniformity made the new checklist easier to use. Ada knew its drawback: a list could not replace experience. She would strive to teach both. Her constancy in returning to the work, instead of the insult, made a strong impression. A dangerous room was by no means improved by making everyone afraid to speak.","Mara used the mechanical models to distract anxious children. They watched planets revolve around the sun, then compared the geo-centric view. The crank's quirk was a squeak on every fourth turn. Cassian applied a little oil while a child explained the model's significance to him with utmost solemnity. He listened as though receiving a naval command.","The promoter sneered at the silliness. Mara told him to leave the children alone. Prudery about laughter was no more appealing than prudery about necklines. The charity did not need his superiority as an entrance fee. His attempt to elicit agreement from the crowd met silence.","'Damn the publicity,' Rowan muttered. His usual civility had reached its limit. Ada looked at him, then at the apprentices. Their uncertain smiles began to sparkle. A little well-timed silliness could help; an inhumane demand to perform happiness would not. Their wish for a safe show was neither unattractive nor weak.","A carpenter arrived and inspected the bulk of the stage. The repair would not be stress-free, he said, but it was practical. The apprentices offered aid. Ada selected tasks according to proficiency rather than physique. An abundance of willing hands was useful only when people knew what to do.","The promoter asked for a vice to hold a damaged bracket. The repair had made the stage stable, but the organizer decided a succession of models circling the courtyard would offer better accessibility. The spectators moved their chairs voluntarily. The sponsors accepted the loss of the raised photographs. Practicality had won a small but satisfying victory. Mara decided another speech would add to nobody's happiness.","Mara wore the restored gown with a new detachable panel. Its design preserved the original fabric without forcing its old shape onto her. Ada called this transferability of skills: conservation knowledge could aid clothing design, while a dressmaker's knowledge of movement could improve a display mount. Mara thought their complementary work deserved more credit than any claim of singular genius.","Cassian wore a plain borrowed waistcoat. Its suitability for breathing had made it the ultimate luxury. He admired Mara with enough openness to make the promoter look away. Thinness, uniformity and the size of a mannequin had little to do with the way she occupied a room. Her beauty included the amusement she was trying to hide.","The populous courtyard began to sparkle with reflected light. A parallel row of lanterns led towards the guild's rooms. The old port's significance to the tourism industry was easy to sell; its young workers' need for space was less photogenic. Tonight the guild could show both. No one had to pretend the housing crisis had vanished to enjoy the clothes.","Ada spoke briefly. The apprentices' proficiency reflected practice, not a rare inherited gift. Their stamina had been tested, and the severity of the day's near accident would be reviewed. She would strive for a better setup next time. Her simplicity of manner gave the promise weight. Mara preferred it to a declaration of superiority.","The guitarist played faster, inviting an acceleration in the dancers' steps. Mara refused to hurry. A stress-free minute with Cassian, plus a glass of cold water, seemed far more attractive. An unattractive speech could wait. The alternative was similar to work, and she had had enough of that for one evening.","She did not claim that increased productivity was the only aim of training. The prevalence of unpaid rehearsal work had put poorer students at a disadvantage. Ada would review that with the sponsors. Her realism included money, and the apprentices seemed to be relieved that somebody had said so. Cautiousness about promises did not require abandoning ambition.","The promoter had returned the disputed certificate and promised a replacement invoice. Its validity remained to be checked. His attempted deceit might have cost the event money even without an injury. The organizer would consider the matter with advisers. Nobody could establish corruption merely from a bad label, but they could stop using that label now.","The promoter's aggressiveness had one useful consequence: it made the need for rigour visible. The prevalence of copied certificates was no excuse for accepting this one. Ada asked for random checks of future deliveries, not a supplier selected randomly from an unknown list. The difference mattered. Her insistence made the organiser's weak point easier to repair.","At the children's table, the model planets continued to revolve. The geo-centric view stood beside the newer system. Cassian had used a clamp to correct its wobble, and Rowan had finally stopped making jokes about vice. A little girl asked whether a globe could be wrong and still be beautiful. Mara said it could, provided its label explained what it represented.","The exhibit's paired arrangement had a pleasing symmetry. A brass ring carried a faint green tint. Its trademark was worn but legible. Mara took the utmost care not to touch the painted surface as she adjusted the label. This was no triviality to the owner, who had lent a family object for strangers to enjoy.","That answer pleased Ada. The rarity of the old model did not make its astronomy current; its age did give it another kind of significance. Conformity to modern science was not the only basis for preserving an object. Truthfulness about its history mattered as well. Mara liked the practical depth of that distinction.","The old model and its modern analogue showed a similarity of workmanship without agreement about the universe. Transferability of a technique did not guarantee transferability of a theory. Ada found that distinction useful for her own teaching. The aspiring maker could learn precision from the object without copying its astronomy.","The promoter tried to reclaim the microphone. His repeated demands had begun to overwhelm even his own assistant. She asked him to wait. Notwithstanding his impatience, she would finish the announcements first. A tiny rebellion, perhaps, but the room's response made it less lonely.","Cassian took Mara outside before another request found her. A quirk in the gate made it stick. He held it open while she passed; she laid a hand on his chest as she did. The promised evening's predictability had suffered, but she found she preferred its surprises.","“I have no idea what I did to deserve you,” he said. “Some memorable sin, perhaps.” She laughed and pulled him closer. For once he accepted a mysterious explanation without asking for papers.","Cassian's physique had made one waistcoat fail; his spontaneity had rescued the day from becoming solemn. Mara could not resist teasing him about both. 'It is not ruined at all,' he said, holding up the repaired garment. 'It has merely developed a moral objection to buttons.' His proud face was a temptation she made no effort to resist.","“You were wise to borrow that,” she said, touching the waistcoat.","“I was wise to marry its critic.”","She laughed at the silliness. His prudence had never been complete where she was concerned, and her own prudery was hardly improved by moonlight. She drew him close enough to test the new waistcoat's strength. It survived. The same could not be said for his carefully prepared reply."],"questions":[],"wordCount":980,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0144\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Previous</button><button data-go=\"144\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x017c01","title":"The Lighthouse Paints Back","volume":"婚后外传：灯塔不肯重新化妆","anchor":"chapter-0145","paragraphs":["The lighthouse keeper greeted Mara with a purple hand and Cassian with a pot of gray paint. He had mistaken their arrival for the arrival of decorators. Cassian accepted the pot. Mara accepted the explanation. Neither accepted the keeper's claim that the ancient wall painting could be restored before tea.","“Goodness,” he said. “You mean it's complicated?”","The mural's excellence lay partly in its restrained pastel colours. Its high quality had drawn visitors for a century. The tourist industry now wanted brighter photographs, and a committee had approved repainting. Its popularity had apparently become an excuse to erase its shortcoming: the colours did not appear vivid on cheap postcards.","Mara surveyed the height of the wall and the radius of the scaffold's working space. She needed to ascertain the stability of every platform before climbing. The keeper had shown foresight in keeping old photographs. His caution ended there. A workman had already covered one corner with modern paint. Its strong odor made Cassian open the door.","The keeper's individualism had once made him refuse an electric kettle. Now he owned three. His tolerance for modern life varied according to the amount of tea it offered. Mara admired the humanity behind that practical morality. Cassian admired the elegance of a compromise that kept everyone warm. A flexible personality might be as useful as a clever plan.","A local conservator named Ivo arrived with infrared photographs. Their intensity could be adjusted to reveal differences beneath the surface, but the images did not identify every material. Reproducibility would depend on controlled lighting and a careful record. Ivo asked his trainee to repeat the setup. The regularity of those small steps protected them from mistaking a camera effect for an old line.","A lamp could magnify the glare on the surface, making a faint line disappear. Ivo changed its angle, then asked the trainee to call out each setting. Various images initially looked alike. The credibility of his comparison would rest on recording the difference, not on his capability to sound certain. That struck Mara as wise.","The committee chair accused him of incompetence. He replied with unexpected humility: he had made mistakes before and learned to check. His loyalty was to the wall, not his own first impression. Mara admired that trait. Personality could make an expert pleasant; trustworthiness needed something more.","The chair's awful sales pitch had promised instant results. Her selling point was speed, not an ability to nurture the old material. She wanted not the repair but rather the spectacle. Mara recognized the extreme claim as an invitation to disappointment. They needed to persevere with a quieter method, even if it made nobody famous.","Outside, the weather changed rapidly. Fog reduced visibility. A shortage of dry storage space meant supplies were stacked in the entrance. The keeper wanted to nourish both the sea birds and visiting children from the same bag of biscuits. Mara offered to buy proper food instead. The keeper's kindness was overwhelming; its practicality needed help.","Quite a few visitors remained outside. A gull began to hover at the open window. The keeper put the biscuits away. Mara asked about the availability of safe rooms if the weather worsened. His answer was vague, though the old charts showed enough floor space. The unpredictability of coastal weather deserved more foresight than that.","At the cliff, a red barrier marked a dangerous section. Falling stone could pose a threat to walkers. The committee wanted it removed because it spoiled photographs. Cassian strongly disagreed. A safety restriction might be unavoidable. Concealing it for a luxury brochure would not improve the cliff.","A heated meeting began in the keeper's kitchen. Every eligible member held a voting right; the chair could not replace that with a show of loyal faces. One fisherman intended to vote against repainting. He called the committee's stance self-contradictory: it praised sustainability while destroying a durable work. A teacher asked for flexible plans that would nurture apprentices rather than turn them into cheap labour.","The trust's lawyer was seeking an urgent hearing at the High Court about ownership of the mural. The court's jurisdiction was disputed; justice would not be delivered by an argument over sandwiches. Meanwhile, nobody should remove pieces. Mara wanted to get rid of the misconception that restoration was simply repainting.","A reporter called the coming delay proof of laziness. Cassian gave him a look. Mara answered first. “Not laziness, but rather patience.”","Mara heard the keeper cry out a warning as the child approached the cliff. She turned before she knew what he had seen.","Ivo added a note to his condition sheet. Endurance of old paint depended partly on its environment. The feasibility of treating it here required tests. This was his speciality, not a performance of superiority. His temperament remained calm even when the chair's hostility rose.","From outside came a cry. A delivery cart had rolled towards the barrier. Cassian ran, caught the handle and turned it before it struck a child. Two simultaneous movements followed: Mara pulled the child clear, and the empty cart tipped over the edge.","Its crash came back as an echo from the rocks. The keeper went white. For once, nobody mentioned postcards."],"questions":[],"wordCount":867,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0145\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：灯塔不肯重新化妆</div><h1>The Lighthouse Paints Back</h1><p id=\"chapter-0145-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> lighthouse keeper <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3716\" data-words=\"3716\">greeted</a> Mara <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9260\" data-words=\"9260\">with</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6634\" data-words=\"6634\">purple</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3789\" data-words=\"3789\">hand</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> Cassian <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-9260\" data-words=\"9260\">with</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6328\" data-words=\"6328\">pot</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3702\" data-words=\"3702\">gray</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5926\" data-words=\"5926\">paint</a>. <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3847\" data-words=\"3847\">He</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5281\" data-words=\"5281,5280\">mistaken</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8424\" data-words=\"8424\">their</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-511\" data-words=\"511\">arrival</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3344\" data-words=\"3344\">for</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-511\" data-words=\"511\">arrival</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> decorators. shirt torn at the shoulder. Mara had quite a few opinions about that shirt. She settled for putting a hand against his chest until her pulse slowed. His considerate silence lasted almost a minute.","“Have I lost eligibility for tea?” he asked.","She kissed him hard enough to end the joke, then checked the scrape. Behind them, the keeper sat with the frightened child. The child's bravery had outlasted the first shock. Now he cried, and Mara was glad nobody demanded further courage from him.","The accident rapidly changed the argument. The faulty brake could pose a threat to anyone on the path. The keeper strongly supported an unavoidable closure until repairs were complete. A simultaneous inspection of the cliff barrier found another loose fixing. Cassian offered to arrange equipment, but refused to let volunteers substitute endurance for a safe working platform.","Cassian wanted to get rid of the damaged cart immediately. Mara reminded him to preserve it for the safety inspection first. Its awful brake needed checking, and the worker's competence should not be judged from the chair's anger. Her allegation of incompetence had arrived more quickly than any attempt to understand what happened.","The chair still wanted to open. A luxury launch, she said, would nourish the local economy. Ivo questioned the feasibility of guests using a route they had just seen fail. The event's selling point was the view. Fog had removed visibility anyway. Its unpredictability was precisely why a plan needed alternatives.","Mara helped move supplies into dry storage. The cart's design had one obvious shortcoming: its handle could release the brake unintentionally. Ivo recorded the height and radius of its movement space. The keeper could not remember who had last checked it. Goodness alone did not create a maintenance record. Neither did an abundance of posters about excellence.","Inside, the mural remained stable. Ivo compared the infrared images under controlled intensity. His trainee found the same line twice. That reproducibility gave them a firmer basis for examining it. They would not magnify its meaning into proof of a whole lost composition. Its most useful lesson concerned the corner beneath the purple overpaint.","The wall's high quality survived the latest interference. Ivo's speciality gave him an eye for small changes; his assistant's foresight had saved a clean reference image. Together they could assess its stability. The paint's odor remained strong in one corner, so Cassian helped air the room while keeping visitors out.","The chair demanded a brighter style. Her preference for popularity had become a rule about value. Mara liked the old wall's restrained personality and the pastel sea behind its painted boats. A distinctive temperament belonged to a work as much as its maker. The restoration could retain that trait without freezing every stain forever.","Ivo's humility made the committee more willing to listen. He admitted the material's condition created an extreme challenge. Their options involved a shortage of staff time and the need for dry access. His initiative was to test a tiny area, review the result and then decide. The regularity of that process mattered more than dramatic confidence.","A call came from the lawyer. The High Court had permitted preservation of the disputed object while documents were examined; it had not resolved final ownership. The order's limits did not strike Mara as a weakness. Its modest scope made it useful. The committee could not manufacture jurisdiction by voting loudly, nor reduce justice to an afternoon victory.","The fisherman kept his voting right and his intention to vote against repainting. His stance was not hostility to tourism. He hoped the tourist industry would support a place people actually lived. Solidarity among the keepers and boat crews made practical work possible. It was humanity, not a birthright of wealthy visitors, that gave the place its appeal.","The committee needed a style of leadership with more tolerance for unwelcome facts. The noisiness of an argument did not establish a moral position. Mara found the chair's promise of community individualism self-contradictory when she would not hear a single dissenting member. A little fairness could begin with letting the fisherman finish.","Cassian had initially mistaken Ivo's reserved disposition for distance. Now he saw the care beneath it. The conservator's loyalty and trustworthiness showed in what he recorded when nobody was looking. That impressed Cassian more than the committee's magnificence of language. The ceiling's leaking patch had no patience for speeches.","His strongly worded letter had seemed overwhelming at first. Now the fisherman could add his name to the appeal without feeling alone. He remained eligible for the vote. A promise of solidarity could not decide every detail of sustainability, but it made waiting easier. The men agreed to keep one another informed.","A gull began to hover outside, hoping for the keeper's biscuits. Mara offered the bird nothing and the keeper a sandwich. Nourishing them differently seemed the first sensible reform of the day. Cassian watched her, his tired face unexpectedly tender."],"questions":[],"wordCount":838,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0146\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：灯塔不肯重新化妆</div><h1>A Duke Covered in Dust</h1><p id=\"chapter-0146-p1\">Cassian <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7896\" data-words=\"7896\">stood</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-593\" data-words=\"593\">at</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1511\" data-words=\"1511\">cliff</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4167\" data-words=\"4167\">in</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1533\" data-words=\"1533\">cloud</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3702\" data-words=\"3702\">gray</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2618\" data-words=\"2618\">dust</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3941\" data-words=\"3941,3907\">his</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7497\" data-words=\"7497\">shirt</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8310\" data-words=\"8310\">torn</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-593\" data-words=\"593\">at</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7522\" data-words=\"7522\">shoulder</a>. Cassian stayed below with a tray, prepared to catch nothing heavier than a brush. She looked down at him. “You could have gone home.”","“And miss being ordered about from a great height?”","The work was modest. Ivo's test indicated that a small patch of overpaint might be removed without harming the layer beneath. He would assess it over days, not promise a transformation in minutes. Mara helped record the area, then handed back the tools. She liked the restraint of a task finished at its proper stopping point.","The trust had agreed to postpone the opening. The decision was overwhelming, once members saw the path and the mural. The chair chose not to vote against a delay. Her ability to change a position mattered more than a final show of defiance. The artist's wall would keep its quiet sea for now.","Outside, repair crews worked rapidly on the barrier. Its damaged section would continue to pose a threat to walkers until the fixing was replaced. The closure was unavoidable. The keeper's simultaneous offers of tea and unsolicited help slowed the workers, so Mara gave him a different job: feed people indoors. He accepted with relief.","The High Court hearing about ownership would continue next month. The lawyer asked that records be kept and the parties avoid altering the disputed work. Cassian listened carefully, then put the letter away. They could preserve the wall while waiting. They did not need to rehearse the legal dispute at every meal.","The keeper made soup. His desire to nourish everyone in reach had finally found a task large enough to contain it. Mara told him to get rid of the stale biscuits. The gulls protested from the roof, a committee with no constitutional limits. Cassian admired their commitment while firmly closing the kitchen window.","The keeper took the initiative to invite the trainee back. His style of teaching was informal, but he hoped to nurture real curiosity. He called the invitation solidarity, not charity. Mara liked his flexible response. An accusation of laziness had struck her as a mistake from the start; his energy now made the error obvious. She suspected his morality was simpler than the committee's: feed people, keep the light on, return borrowed tools.","A school group arrived for an alternative tour. The pupils saw photographs of the infrared setup and the tested patch. The trainee explained what had changed between observations. He was proud without pretending he had solved everything. Mara knew that balance would take practice. His first success might help him persevere through the next disappointing result.","The trainee asked the pupils to call out the boats they could see. A small boy wanted to cry out each answer before his sister. Mara held up a hand. There was no shortage of time. Their lesson was not a contest but rather a shared game, and both would have a turn.","The keeper's granddaughter wanted to cry out when she saw the old boat beneath the modern paint. Mara asked her to look closely before celebrating. A tiny recovered line could be exciting in its own right. It need not become a whole ship in a story told too soon. The girl nodded, then smiled again with undiminished pleasure.","Cassian carried the used tea things to the kitchen. The room had acquired various extra occupants: a wet fisherman, a child drawing boats, the trainee and a dog nobody admitted owning. Their noisiness made conversation difficult and the old house feel alive. Mara decided the keeper's greatest luxury was this easy company.","The committee had expected magnificence, not a lesson in maintenance. Yet the temporary tour showed visitors something the opening brochure had missed. A lighthouse survived because people cleaned lamps, repaired paths, kept records and returned when the weather was awful. The place did not rest on one donor's approval.","A child made a ball bounce along the kitchen floor until the keeper rescued his soup. The height of the bounce suggested more energy than the room could safely contain. Cassian proposed a supervised walk instead. For once he had a sensible answer that did not involve purchasing a building.","At dusk Cassian took Mara out to a safe viewpoint. The path remained damp, but the clouds were opening. The sea reflected a long strip of pale sky. She leaned back against him and let the warmth of his body answer the cold.","Mara could endure the cold a little longer. The beauty outside had another dimension now: she knew the people who kept its light working. She strongly preferred this to a perfect postcard. Cassian's hand closed warmly over hers, and for once his desire to protect her did not strike her as excessive.","“Think about a quiet weekend,” he murmured.","“Only if you promise not to pitch it as a rescue operation.”","He laughed and kissed the hair above her ear. Below, the lighthouse lamp began its steady turn. Mara watched its light sweep the water and found, at last, that she had nothing urgent to add."],"questions":[],"wordCount":853,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0147\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：灯塔不肯重新化妆</div><h1>The Lighthouse Keeps Its Face</h1><p id=\"chapter-0147-p1\">Mara <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1515\" data-words=\"1515\">climbed</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> scaffold <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5720\" data-words=\"5720\">once</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4568\" data-words=\"4568\">it</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-792\" data-words=\"792\">been</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4406\" data-words=\"4406\">inspected</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6542\" data-words=\"6542\">properly</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7346\" data-words=\"7346\">secured</a>.
